name="us-gaap:NatureOfOperations" id="f-229" continuedAt="f-229-1" escape="true">General information</ix:nonNumeric></span></div><ix:continuation id="f-229-1"><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Seadrill Limited (along with any one or more of its consolidated subsidiaries, or to all such entities, referred to as "</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Seadrill</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">", "</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">we</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">", "</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">us</span><span </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Company</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">") is incorporated in Bermuda. We are an offshore drilling contractor providing worldwide offshore drilling services to the oil and gas industry. Our primary business is the ownership and operation of drillships and semi-submersible rigs for operations in shallow to ultra-deepwater areas in both benign and harsh environments. We contract our drilling units to drill wells for our customers on a dayrate basis. Our customers include oil super-majors, state-owned national oil companies and independent oil and gas companies. In addition, we provide management services to certain affiliated entities.</span></div><ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-1" name="us-gaap:BasisOfAccountingPolicyPolicyTextBlock" id="f-230" escape="true"><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Basis of presentation</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">The accompanying unaudited Condensed Consolidated Financial Statements of Seadrill Limited have been prepared in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the U.S. ("</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">U.S. GAAP</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">") for interim financial information and with the instructions to Form 10-Q and Article 10 of Regulation S-X of the SEC. Accordingly, pursuant to such rules and regulations, they do not include all disclosures required by U.S. GAAP for annual financial statements. In the opinion of management, the unaudited Condensed Consolidated Financial Statements includes all adjustments (consisting of normal recurring adjustments) necessary for a fair statement of financial position, results of operations and cash flows for the interim periods. </span></div><div><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Results of operations for interim periods are not necessarily indicative of results of operations for the respective full years, or any future period. The Incremental Notes were issued at <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-71" decimals="INF" name="sdrl:DebtInstrumentIssuedAsAPercentOfPar" scale="-2" id="f-370">100.75</ix:nonFraction>% of par.</span></div><div><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Revolving credit facility</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">On July 27, 2023, Seadrill, along with its subsidiary, Seadrill Finance, entered into a Senior Secured Revolving Credit Agreement (as amended, the "</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Credit Agreemen</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">t"), which established a Senior Secured Revolving Credit Facility (the "</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Revolving Credit Facility</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">"). The commitments under the Revolving Credit Facility, which carries a <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-72" name="us-gaap:DebtInstrumentTerm" format="ixt-sec:durwordsen" id="f-371">five-year</ix:nonNumeric> term, became available for drawdown on July 27, 2023. The Revolving Credit Facility permits borrowings of up to $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-73" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LineOfCreditFacilityMaximumBorrowingCapacity" scale="6" id="f-372">225</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million in revolving credit for working capital and other corporate purposes and includes an "accordion feature" allowing Seadrill to increase this limit by up to an additional $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-73" decimals="-6" name="sdrl:LineOfCreditFacilityAccordionFeatureIncreaseLimit" scale="6" id="f-373">100</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million, subject to agreement from the lenders. As of March 31, 2026, it also included a provision for issuing letters of credit up to $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-74" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LongTermDebt" scale="6" id="f-374">50</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million. On April 3, 2026, the Company amended the Credit Agreement to increase the letter of credit sub-limit from $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-74" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LongTermDebt" scale="6" id="f-375">50</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million to $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-75" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LongTermDebt" scale="6" id="f-376">100</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">The Revolving Credit Facility incurs interest at a rate equal to a specified margin plus, at Seadrill Finance&#8217;s option, either: (i) the Term Secured Overnight Financing Rate ("</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">SOFR</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">") Rate (as defined in the Credit Agreement) plus <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-76" decimals="4" name="us-gaap:DebtInstrumentBasisSpreadOnVariableRate1" scale="-2" id="f-377">0.10</ix:nonFraction>%; or (ii) the Daily Simple SOFR (as defined in the Credit Agreement) plus <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-77" decimals="4" name="us-gaap:DebtInstrumentBasisSpreadOnVariableRate1" scale="-2" id="f-378">0.10</ix:nonFraction>%. For both the Term SOFR Rate loans and Daily Simple SOFR loans, the applicable margin was <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-78" decimals="4" name="us-gaap:DebtInstrumentBasisSpreadOnVariableRate1" scale="-2" id="f-379">2.75</ix:nonFraction>% per annum as of March 31, 2026, and may vary based on Seadrill&#8217;s Credit Ratings (as defined in the Credit Agreement), from <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-79" decimals="4" name="us-gaap:DebtInstrumentBasisSpreadOnVariableRate1" scale="-2" id="f-380">2.50</ix:nonFraction>% to <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-80" decimals="4" name="us-gaap:DebtInstrumentBasisSpreadOnVariableRate1" scale="-2" id="f-381">3.50</ix:nonFraction>% per annum. A commitment fee is incurred under the Revolving Credit Facility on undrawn amounts, at a rate of <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-81" decimals="3" name="us-gaap:LineOfCreditFacilityUnusedCapacityCommitmentFeePercentage" scale="-2" id="f-382">0.5</ix:nonFraction>% per annum to and including July 27, 2026, <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-82" decimals="4" name="us-gaap:LineOfCreditFacilityUnusedCapacityCommitmentFeePercentage" scale="-2" id="f-383">0.75</ix:nonFraction>% per annum from and including July 28, 2026 to and including July 27, 2027, and <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-83" decimals="4" name="us-gaap:LineOfCreditFacilityUnusedCapacityCommitmentFeePercentage" scale="-2" id="f-384">1.00</ix:nonFraction>% per annum thereafter. This facility has not been drawn to date.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">During the third quarter of 2025, the Company issued a NOK<ix:nonFraction unitRef="nok" contextRef="c-84" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:GuaranteeObligationsMaximumExposure" scale="6" id="f-385">403</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million guarantee ($<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-85" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:GuaranteeObligationsMaximumExposure" scale="6" id="f-386"><ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-85" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:GuaranteeObligationsMaximumExposure" scale="6" id="f-387">41</ix:nonFraction></ix:nonFraction>&#160;million as of March&#160;31, 2026) under the Revolving Credit Facility related to the SFL Hercules Ltd. claim. As of March&#160;31, 2026, outstanding letters of credit and bank guarantees under the Revolving Credit Facility totaled approximately $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-86" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LettersOfCreditOutstandingAmount" scale="6" id="f-388">47</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million, which reduced the Company&#8217;s available capacity under the Revolving Credit Facility to $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-87" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LineOfCreditFacilityMaximumBorrowingCapacity" scale="6" id="f-389">178</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">For further details, please refer to Note 13 </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">&#8211; continuedAt="f-431-1" escape="true">Commitments and contingencies </ix:nonNumeric></span></div><ix:continuation id="f-431-1" continuedAt="f-431-2"><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span economic benefits will be required to settle an obligation and the amount is reasonably estimable. An adverse outcome in a matter described below could have an adverse effect on Seadrill's operating results, cash flows and financial position. Accruals for contingencies and uncertain tax positions related to matters described below, if any, are recorded in "Accrued expenses" within "Other current liabilities" and "Uncertain tax positions" within "Other non-current liabilities", respectively, in the unaudited Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Legal Proceedings</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">SFL Hercules Ltd.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">In March 2023, Seadrill was served with a claim from SFL Hercules Ltd., filed in the Oslo District Court in Norway, relating to our redelivery of the rig </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">West Hercules</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%"> to SFL Corporation Ltd. ("</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:112%">SFL</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">") in December 2022. In February 2025, the Oslo District Court delivered a judgment in favor of SFL Hercules Ltd. ordering Seadrill to pay SFL approximately $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-107" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LitigationSettlementAmountAwardedToOtherParty" scale="6" id="f-432">37</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million, plus interest and legal costs of approximately $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-107" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LitigationSettlementExpense" scale="6" id="f-433">11</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million, based on foreign exchange rates at the time of the judgment. Seadrill intends to vigorously contest the judgment and filed an appeal in March 2025. The appeal proceedings commenced in April 2026, and we expect the court to issue judgment in the second half of 2026. The ultimate amount due, if any, cannot be predicted at this time.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">As is customary in Norway, Seadrill issued a guarantee in the amount of NOK<ix:nonFraction unitRef="nok" contextRef="c-108" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:GuaranteeObligationsMaximumExposure" scale="6" id="f-434">574</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million in August 2025 (approximately $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-109" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:GuaranteeObligationsMaximumExposure" scale="6" id="f-435">59</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million as of March&#160;31, 2026) to the Norwegian Enforcement Authorities as security for the judgment. The issued guarantee consists of NOK<ix:nonFraction unitRef="nok" contextRef="c-84" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:GuaranteeObligationsMaximumExposure" scale="6" id="f-436">403</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million (approximately $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-85" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:GuaranteeObligationsMaximumExposure" scale="6" id="f-437"><ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-85" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:GuaranteeObligationsMaximumExposure" scale="6" id="f-438">41</ix:nonFraction></ix:nonFraction>&#160;million as of March&#160;31, 2026) from our Revolving Credit Facility and NOK<ix:nonFraction unitRef="nok" contextRef="c-110" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:GuaranteeObligationsMaximumExposure" scale="6" id="f-439">171</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million (approximately $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-111" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:GuaranteeObligationsMaximumExposure" scale="6" id="f-440">18</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million as of March&#160;31, 2026) from our Bilateral Facility (as defined herein).</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Sonadrill fees claim</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">In March 2023, Seadrill was served with a claim from an individual (the "</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Claimant</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">") filed in the High Court of Justice, Business and Property Courts of England and Wales, King's Bench Division, Commercial Court (the "</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">High Court</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">"). The Claimant alleged breach of contract and unjust enrichment damages of approximately $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-112" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LossContingencyDamagesSoughtValue" scale="6" id="f-441">72</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million related to an alleged failure by the Company to pay the Claimant a fee for services in arranging the Sonadrill joint venture dating back to 2018. The case concluded in March 2025.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">In July 2025, the High Court rendered judgment in favor of the Claimant. In October 2025, the High Court ruled on the first tranche of damages for the period prior to October 2024, including interest and legal fees, and Seadrill paid the first tranche of damages of $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-113" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LossContingencyDamagesPaidValue" scale="6" id="f-442">43</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million. The parties are continuing to make submissions to the High Court on the issue of the quantum of damages to be paid for the second tranche of damages incurred subsequent to October 2024; however, Seadrill presently estimates that its aggregate liability following the High Court's judgment, including all interest and legal fees, is unlikely to exceed $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-114" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LossContingencyEstimateOfPossibleLoss" scale="6" id="f-443">61</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million, subject to finalization of calculations relating to second tranche interest. Seadrill's request for permission for an appeal to the Court of Appeal was not granted.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Nigerian Cabotage Act litigation</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">In November 2015, the Nigerian Maritime Administration and Safety Agency ("</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">NMASA</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">") issued a detention in respect of the rig </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">West Capella</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> for failure to comply with requirements of the Coastal and Inland Shipping (Cabotage) Act 2003 (the "</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Cabotage Act</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">"), specifically, failure to pay a Cabotage fee of <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-115" decimals="2" name="sdrl:ContractValueDeductionPercentage" scale="-2" id="f-444">2</ix:nonFraction>% on contract revenue. While the named party is Seadrill Mobile Units Nigeria Ltd (previously an Aquadrill entity, acquired by Seadrill upon the merger of Seadrill and Aquadrill) ("</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">SMUNL</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">"), the matter relates to <ix:nonFraction unitRef="rig" contextRef="c-116" decimals="INF" name="sdrl:NumberOfRigs" format="ixt-sec:numwordsen" scale="0" id="f-445">three</ix:nonFraction> rigs: the </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">West Capella</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">, </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">West Saturn</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> and </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">West Jupiter</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">. SMUNL commenced proceedings in May 2016 against the Honourable Minister for Transportation, the Attorney General of the Federation and NMASA with respect to interpretation of the Cabotage Act. In June 2019, the Federal High Court of Nigeria delivered a judgment (1) finding that: (a) Drilling operations fall within the definition of "Coastal Trade" or "Cabotage" under the Cabotage Act and (b) Drilling Rigs fall within the definition of "Vessels" under the Cabotage Act, and (2) directing SMUNL to deduct <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-115" decimals="2" name="sdrl:ContractValueDeductionPercentage" scale="-2" id="f-446">2</ix:nonFraction>%, or approximately $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-115" decimals="-6" name="sdrl:ContractValueDeductionValue" scale="6" id="f-447">69</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million, of their contract value and remit the same to NMASA. In June 2019, the Court of Appeals sitting in Lagos ("</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">COA</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">") issued a conflicting judgment in </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Transocean Support Services Nigeria &amp; Ors v NIMASA &amp; Anor</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">, finding drilling units cannot be deemed vessels under the Cabotage Act pending appeal. SMUNL filed an appeal to the COA in July 2019, and applied to the Federal High Court for an injunction pending appeal to prevent enforcement. Due to the volume of cases currently being handled by the COA, the Registry of the COA is yet to schedule the hearing date for the appeal. Although we intend to strongly pursue this appeal, we cannot predict the outcome of this case.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Sete Brazil claim</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">In or around 2010, Petroleo Brasileiro S.A. ("</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:112%">Petrobras</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">") initiated a project in Brazil to construct a fleet of <ix:nonFraction unitRef="rig" contextRef="c-117" decimals="INF" name="sdrl:NumberOfRigs" scale="0" id="f-448">28</ix:nonFraction> offshore drilling units to support Petrobras (the "</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:112%">Sete Brazil Project</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">"). A Brazilian company ("</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:112%">Sete Brazil</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">") was formed in Brazil as a vehicle for the Sete Brazil Project. The Sete Brazil Project was unable to obtain financing and none of the <ix:nonFraction unitRef="rig" contextRef="c-117" decimals="INF" name="sdrl:NumberOfRigs" scale="0" id="f-449">28</ix:nonFraction> offshore drilling units was ever constructed. Sete Brazil was eventually declared bankrupt by the Brazilian courts in December 2024 although that bankruptcy is presently suspended. </span></div><div><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">In January 2025, Seadrill Brazil received notices from Petrobras asserting "delay penalties" against Seadrill Brazil relating to <ix:nonFraction unitRef="drillship" contextRef="c-117" decimals="INF" name="sdrl:NumberOfDrillships" format="ixt-sec:numwordsen" scale="0" id="f-450">three</ix:nonFraction> drillships to be constructed under the Sete Brazil Project and operated in Brazil by Seadrill Brazil under contracts awarded in 2012. The aggregate amount of the delay penalties claimed by Petrobras as of the date of receipt of the notices was approximately $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-118" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LossContingencyDamagesSoughtValue" scale="6" id="f-451">213</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million, with the potential for further delay penalties, which could be significant, to be assessed ratably over the remaining term of the drilling contracts for the <ix:nonFraction unitRef="drillship" contextRef="c-117" decimals="INF" name="sdrl:NumberOfDrillships" format="ixt-sec:numwordsen" scale="0" id="f-452">three</ix:nonFraction> drillships. Petrobras indicated it may exercise set-off rights against certain amounts payable to Seadrill Brazil under its contracts with Petrobras for our <ix:nonFraction unitRef="drillship" contextRef="c-119" decimals="INF" name="sdrl:NumberOfDrillships" format="ixt-sec:numwordsen" scale="0" id="f-453">five</ix:nonFraction> drillships (unrelated to the Sete Brazil Project) that are currently operating in Brazil, revenues related to which are included in our backlog as of March&#160;31, 2026. No set-off right has been exercised to date. The contracts limit aggregate delay penalties to <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-117" decimals="2" name="sdrl:LossContingencyDelayPenaltyPercentage" scale="-2" id="f-454">10</ix:nonFraction>% of the total "estimated contract value", as defined in the contracts.</span></div></ix:continuation><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div><span><br/></span></div><div style="height:54pt;position:relative;width:100%"><div style="bottom:0;position:absolute;width:100%"><div style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">12</span></div></div></div><hr style="page-break-after:always"/><div style="min-height:54pt;width:100%"><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:3pt;text-align:center"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">SEADRILL LIMITED</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:3pt;text-align:center"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">NOTES TO UNAUDITED CONDENSED CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS</span></div></div><ix:continuation id="f-431-2"><div><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">The Sete Brazil Project contracts also provide an alternative remedy to Petrobras of "compensatory damages" based upon termination of the contracts for which we would have joint and several liability if such damages were awarded to Petrobras. Petrobras could seek delay penalties or compensatory damages but could not seek both under the contracts. We were copied on correspondence between Petrobras and Sete Brazil (and certain of its related companies) in which Petrobras alleged that it is entitled to collect compensatory damages of approximately $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-120" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LossContingencyDamagesSoughtValue" scale="6" id="f-455">825</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million from these companies. Petrobras has not indicated to us that they intend to pursue these claims against us or set off these claims against our current drilling contracts. We dispute liability and do not believe any damages are due to Petrobras from us, Sete Brazil or any of its related companies in connection with the Sete Brazil Project as either delay penalties or compensatory damages.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">Petrobras and Seadrill have agreed to participate in voluntary mediation, and Petrobras has committed to not exercise any set-off rights pending the outcome of the mediation. Petrobras has indicated that the mediation could commence in the third quarter of 2026, but there could be further delays. We cannot predict when the mediation will be completed, or what the outcome may be. Dialogue between the parties is ongoing. We are evaluating our legal options, which may include, among other things, seeking injunctive relief, seeking remedies against Petrobras under Seadrill&#8217;s prior U.S. Chapter 11 bankruptcy proceedings, and asserting counterclaims for substantial damages against Petrobras in Brazilian courts. This matter is in its early stages, and we are not able to predict its timing or outcome. In addition, the nature, timing, calculation and ultimate amount of the purported penalties are subject to principles of contract interpretation before Brazilian courts. Seadrill intends to vigorously defend its position and pursue available remedies.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">Because we do not believe that it is probable that a loss with respect to the claims alleged by Petrobras has been incurred, and we cannot reasonably estimate the amount of any such loss, were it to be incurred, we have not accrued any amounts in respect thereof in our financial statements.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Brazil tax audit</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Seadrill Servi&#231;os de Petr&#243;leo Ltda ("</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Seadrill Brazil</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">") has a long-standing dispute with Brazil&#8217;s tax authority relating to assessment of income tax, penalties, and interest for years 2009 and 2010 and is litigating the matter in Brazil&#8217;s courts. The trial court ruled in favor of Seadrill Brazil, but the Federal Regional Court reversed the lower court decision in September 2023 and upheld the tax authority&#8217;s assessment. In the first quarter of 2024, Seadrill Brazil filed an appeal in Brazil&#8217;s Superior Court of Justice, and we continue to vigorously defend our position. The ultimate timing and outcome of this litigation cannot be determined. As of March&#160;31, 2026, the assessed income tax, penalties, interest accruing on the asserted tax underpayment, and other amounts the courts potentially may award Brazil&#8217;s government, together, totaled approximately $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-121" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LossContingencyDamagesSoughtValue" scale="6" id="f-456">83</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">In connection with its judicial appeal against the tax authority&#8217;s assessment for years 2009 and 2010, Seadrill Brazil has entered into an agreement for an insurance bond of BRL<ix:nonFraction unitRef="brl" contextRef="c-122" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LossContingencyDamagesSoughtValue" scale="6" id="f-457">435</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million ($<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-122" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LossContingencyDamagesSoughtValue" scale="6" id="f-458">83</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million as of March&#160;31, 2026).</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">Additionally, Seadrill Brazil has brought administrative appeals against assessments of additional income tax, indirect taxes, penalties, and interest for years 2012, 2016, and 2017. The assessments for these subsequent years raise issues similar to those that are the subject of the disputed assessments for 2009 and 2010, but the 2012 assessment involves other factual and legal issues as well. The timing and outcome of these administrative appeals and of any subsequent judicial review cannot be determined. As of March&#160;31, 2026, the assessed taxes, penalties, and interest for 2012, 2016, and 2017 totaled, in aggregate, approximately $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-123" decimals="-6" name="us-gaap:LossContingencyDamagesSoughtValue" scale="6" id="f-459">89</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Other matters</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Other tax audits and disputed assessments of income and other taxes, including applicable penalties and interest, remain outstanding as of March&#160;31, 2026 and continue to be monitored and evaluated by the Company. These tax audit and assessment claims are attributable principally to Ghana, Mexico, Norway, and the United States. We continue to vigorously defend our tax positions and currently consider the ultimate resolution of tax audit and assessment claims will not have a material adverse effect on our financial position, operating results and cash flows.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">We operate in various countries in the world. We recognize uncertain tax positions if the weight of available evidence indicates that it is more likely than not that the position will be sustained on audit by relevant tax authorities, including resolution of related appeals or litigation processes, if any. While we believe we have the required support for the positions taken on our tax returns, we cannot predict with certainty as to the ultimate outcome of any existing or future assessments. </span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt;text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Other material disputes or litigation</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt;text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">In addition to the foregoing, from time to time we are a named defendant or party in certain other lawsuits, claims or proceedings arising in the ordinary course of business or in connection with our acquisition and disposal activities. Although the outcome of such lawsuits or other proceedings cannot be predicted with certainty, and the amount of any liability that could arise with respect to such lawsuits or other proceedings cannot be predicted accurately, we currently do not expect these other matters to have a material adverse effect on our financial position, operating results and cash flows.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Guarantees</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">We have issued performance guarantees for potential liabilities that may result from drilling activities under current or previous managed rig arrangements with Sonadrill. Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Management's Discussion and Analysis of Financial Condition and Results of Operations</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">The following discussion should be read in conjunction with the unaudited Condensed Consolidated Financial Statements and related notes included in Part I, Item 1. "Financial Statements" of this Quarterly Report on Form 10-Q, as well as the Consolidated Financial Statements and related notes included in our 2025 10-K. </span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">The following discussion and analysis contains forward-looking statements that involve risks and uncertainties. Our actual results may differ materially from those anticipated in these forward-looking statements as a result of certain factors, including those set forth under "Risk Factors" in Part I, Item 1A. of our 2025 10-K and "Forward-Looking Statements" in this Quarterly Report on Form 10-Q. </span></div><div id="i9c220a2869cd46b1a4faef58f4710c45_109"></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Our Business</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">We are an offshore drilling contractor providing worldwide offshore drilling services to the oil and gas industry. Our primary business is the ownership and operation of drillships and semi-submersible rigs for operations in shallow to ultra-deepwater in both benign and harsh environments. We contract our drilling units to drill wells for our customers on a dayrate basis. Our customers include oil super-majors, state-owned national oil companies and independent oil and gas companies. In addition, we provide management services to certain affiliated entities.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">As of March&#160;31, 2026, we owned a total of 15 drilling rigs. In addition to our owned assets, as of March&#160;31, 2026, we managed two 7th generation drillships owned by Sonangol EP.</span></div><div id="i9c220a2869cd46b1a4faef58f4710c45_112"></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Significant Developments</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Oil price volatility</span></div><div><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">The price of oil has experienced increased volatility and has risen in response to the ongoing conflicts in the Middle East, including the current conflict in Iran, which started on February 28, 2026, and the unprecedented closure of the Strait of Hormuz. The Brent oil price was $71 per barrel on February 27, 2026 and increased to an average price of approximately $103 per barrel for the month of March 2026. We continue to evaluate and monitor the impacts of the recent oil price volatility and the ongoing conflicts in the Middle East on our business and operations; however, it is not possible to predict the long-term impact, if any, of the disruptions to commodity prices, global energy supplies, energy markets and economic conditions, on our business and operations.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">U.S. global trade policy changes</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Ongoing and recently proposed changes to U.S. global trade policy, along with potential international retaliatory measures, have continued to cause high volatility in global markets and uncertainty around short- and long-term economic impacts in the U.S., including concerns over inflation, recession and slowing growth. We continue to evaluate and monitor the potential impacts of these changes and measures, including the imposition of tariffs and ongoing legal challenges to such tariffs, on our business and operations; however, it is not possible to predict the impact, if any, of any changes or proposed changes to the U.S. global trade policy, or any international retaliatory measures, on our business and operations.</span></div><div id="i9c220a2869cd46b1a4faef58f4710c45_115"></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Contract Backlog </span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Contract backlog includes all firm contracts at the contractual operating dayrate multiplied by the number of days remaining in the firm contract period. For contracts which include a market indexed rate mechanism, we utilize the current applicable dayrate multiplied by the number of days remaining in the firm contract period. Contract backlog includes management contract revenues and leasing revenues from bareboat charter arrangements, denoted as "other" in the tables below. Contract backlog excludes revenues for mobilization, demobilization and contract preparation or other incentive provisions and excludes backlog relating to non-consolidated entities. </span></div><div colspan="3" style="display:none"/></tr></table><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Source: Bloomberg</span></div><div><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">In recent years, oil prices have generally remained at levels that support offshore exploration and development activity, where global rig demand has been steady. This level of demand was sustained by the combination of commodity prices, heightened focus on energy security, and relative attractiveness of offshore plays with respect to both cost and carbon emissions. Recently, however, the ongoing conflict in Iran and the unprecedented closure of the Strait of Hormuz have caused significant disruption in the normal flow of oil, refined petroleum products, and related commodities, resulting in higher oil prices.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">The price of Brent oil averaged $78 per barrel during the three months ended March 31, 2026 up from $68 per barrel in 2025, driven primarily by ongoing conflicts in the Middle East that disrupted global oil supply during the first quarter of 2026.</span></div><div><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Uncertainty persists in the market, particularly in light of concerns over global economic conditions (including the current conflict in Iran), government trade policies and output increases by the Organization of the Petroleum Exporting Countries and other major international producers. This has led to the continued deferral of offshore capital expenditures and could have a negative impact on near-term future demand for offshore drilling services. In addition, inflationary pressures may impact the cost base in our industry, including personnel costs and the prices of goods and services required to reactivate or operate rigs.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">As global tendering activity accelerates, we see signs that point towards a market recovery in 2027. In addition, we believe oil majors are calling for renewed focus on large-scale exploration and investment, and there is also growing consensus that U.S. shale production is plateauing. As a result, with projections of growing oil and gas demand and the lagging energy transition, operators are pivoting back towards deepwater exploration in order to replace reserves and sustain production growth.</span></div><div><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">The table below shows the global number of rigs on Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">a) Contract revenues</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="background-color:#ffffff;color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Contract revenues represent the revenues we earn from contracting our drilling units to customers, primarily on a dayrate basis, and are predominately driven by the average number of rigs under contract during a period, the average dayrates earned and economic utilization achieved by those rigs under contract. We have set out movements in these key indicators of performance in the sections below.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt;padding-left:18pt;text-indent:-18pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">i.</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%;padding-left:13.25pt">Average number of rigs on contract</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">We calculate the average number of rigs on contract by dividing the aggregate days our rigs (excluding managed rigs) were on contract during the reporting period by the number of days in that reporting period.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">The average number of rigs on contract remained consistent at nine in each of the three months ended March 31, 2026 and 2025; however, there was a decrease in the total days on contract resulting in lower contract revenues of $11&#160;million in the three months ended March 31, 2026 compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025. </span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">The decrease was primarily driven by fewer operating days on the </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">West Jupiter</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">,</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> West Capella</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> and </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Sevan Louisiana</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">, which were undergoing contract preparation activities during the three months ended March 31, 2026 for contracts that started during March 2026 in Brazil, Malaysia and the U.S. Gulf of America, respectively, compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025, during which the rigs were operating for more days. The decrease was partially offset by the </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">West Neptune</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> and </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">West Polaris</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">, operating throughout the three months ended March 31, 2026, compared to being partially contracted during the three months ended March 31, 2025.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt;padding-left:18pt;text-indent:-18pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">ii.</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%;padding-left:10.75pt">Average contractual dayrates</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">We calculate the average contractual dayrate by dividing the aggregate contractual dayrates during a reporting period by the aggregate number of days for the reporting period. </span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">The average contractual dayrate earned during the three months ended March 31, 2026 was $343&#160;thousand compared to $323&#160;thousand during the three months ended March 31, 2025, resulting in a $5&#160;million increase in contract revenues in the three months ended March 31, 2026 compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">The increase was driven by higher dayrates for the </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">West Jupiter</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">,</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> West Auriga</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">,</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> West Polaris</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">,</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> West Tellus</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> and </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">West Carina </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">operating in Brazil, the </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">West Neptune </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">operating in the U.S. Gulf of America, and the </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">West Elara</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> operating in Norway during the three months ended March 31, 2026, compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025. These impacts were partially offset by lower dayrates for the </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">West Vela</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> and </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Sevan Louisiana</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> operating in the U.S. Gulf of America during the three months ended March 31, 2026, compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025.</span></div><div style="height:54pt;position:relative;width:100%"><div style="bottom:0;position:absolute;width:100%"><div style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">16</span></div></div></div><hr style="page-break-after:always"/><div style="min-height:54pt;width:100%"><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt;padding-left:18pt;text-indent:-18pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">iii.</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%;padding-left:8.25pt">Economic utilization for rigs on contract</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">We define economic utilization as dayrate revenue earned during the period, excluding bonuses, divided by the contractual operating dayrate multiplied by the number of days on contract in the period. If a drilling unit earns its full operating dayrate throughout a reporting period, its economic utilization would be 100%. However, there are many situations that give rise to a dayrate being earned that is less than the contractual operating rate, such as planned downtime for maintenance. In such situations, economic utilization reduces below 100%. </span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">The economic utilization for the three months ended March 31, 2026 was 95%, compared to 84% for the three months ended March 31, 2025, resulting in a $36&#160;million increase in contract revenues in the three months ended March 31, 2026 compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025. The increase was primarily due to improved economic utilization on the </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">West Tellus</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">, </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">West Polaris</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">,</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> West Auriga </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">and</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> Sevan Louisiana</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> during the three months ended March 31, 2026 compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt;padding-left:18pt;text-indent:-18pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">iv.</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%;padding-left:9.26pt">Deferred mobilization revenues</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">We receive fees for the mobilization of our rigs, where the associated revenue is recognized ratably over the expected term of the related drilling contract. As a result, we record a contract liability for mobilization fees received, which is amortized ratably to contract revenues as services are rendered over the initial term of the related drilling contract.</span></div><div><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">The amortization of deferred mobilization revenues decreased by $1&#160;million during the three months ended March 31, 2026, compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025. The decrease was primarily attributable to mobilization fees received on the </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">West Capella </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">recognized during the first quarter of 2025.</span></div><div><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">b) Reimbursable revenues</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">We generally receive reimbursements from our customers for the purchase of supplies, equipment, personnel and other services provided at their request in accordance with a drilling contract. We classify such revenues as reimbursable revenues.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">For the three months ended March 31, 2026 and the three months ended March 31, 2025, reimbursable revenues primarily related to rigs managed for the Sonadrill joint venture for long-term maintenance projects on the </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Libongos</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%"> and </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Quenguela</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">, along with reimbursable revenues related to services provided across various customers.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">The $5&#160;million decrease in the three months ended March 31, 2026 compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025 was primarily due to reduced reimbursable services provided to the </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">West Gemini</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">during the first quarter of 2026, compared to the first quarter of 2025.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">2) Operating expenses</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Total operating expenses include vessel and rig style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">a) Vessel and rig operating expenses</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Vessel and rig operating expenses represent the costs we incur to operate a drilling unit that is either in operation or stacked. This includes the remuneration of offshore crews, rig supplies, expenses for repair and maintenance, onshore support costs, and the amortization of deferred mobilization costs. Vessel and rig operating expenses are mainly driven by rig activity. On average, we incur higher vessel and rig operating expenses when a rig is operating compared to when it is stacked. For stacked rigs, we incur higher vessel and rig expenses for warm stacked rigs compared to cold stacked rigs. We incur one-time costs for activities such as preservation and severance when we cold stack a rig. We also incur significant costs when re-activating a rig from cold stack, a proportion of which is expensed as incurred.</span></div><div><span><br/></span></div><div style="height:54pt;position:relative;width:100%"><div style="bottom:0;position:absolute;width:100%"><div style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">17</span></div></div></div><hr style="page-break-after:always"/><div style="min-height:54pt;width:100%"><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div></div><div><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Vessel and rig operating expenses increased by $2&#160;million during the three months ended March 31, 2026 compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025. During the three months ended March 31, 2026, there was an $9&#160;million increase in vessel and rig operating expenses compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025, primarily related to the </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">West Polaris </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">commencing operations in February 2025 in Brazil, along with higher repair and maintenance costs across the fleet. This was partially offset by a $7&#160;million decrease in vessel and rig operating expenses during the three months ended March 31, 2026, attributable to lower deferred mobilization costs related to contracts in Brazil completing during the first quarter of 2026.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">b) Depreciation and amortization</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span 2025.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New earnings from Sonadrill.</span></div><div><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">The decrease in earnings of $4&#160;million for the three months ended March 31, 2026 compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025 was primarily driven by lower operating dayrates for </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Libongos </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New West Gemini</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">,</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">partially offset by an increased dayrate for </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Quenguela, </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">operating in Angola during the three months ended March 31, 2026, compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">.</span></div><div style="height:54pt;position:relative;width:100%"><div style="bottom:0;position:absolute;width:100%"><div style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">18</span></div></div></div><hr style="page-break-after:always"/><div style="min-height:54pt;width:100%"><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">b) Other financial and non-operating items</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Other financial and non-operating items improved by $15&#160;million during the three months ended March 31, 2026 compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025, primarily driven by a provision recognized during the three months ended March 31, 2025 related to assets sold, which did not recur during the three months ended March 31, 2026.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">5) Income tax expense</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Income tax expense consists of taxes currently payable and changes in deferred tax assets and liabilities related to our ownership and operation of drilling units and may vary significantly depending on jurisdictions and contractual arrangements. In most cases, the calculation of taxes is based on net income or deemed income, the latter generally being a function of gross revenue.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">The $8&#160;million increase in tax expense during the three months ended March 31, 2026 compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025 primarily reflects changes in the Company's mix of pre-tax income and loss among tax jurisdictions, as well as lower utilization of tax loss carryforwards in the three months ended March 31, 2026.</span></div><div id="i9c220a2869cd46b1a4faef58f4710c45_136"></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Liquidity and Capital Resources</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">1) Capital allocation framework and Share repurchase program</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">In July 2023, in connection with the issuance of the Notes, Seadrill announced capital allocation principles designed to prioritize a conservative capital structure and liquidity position, focused capital investment in its fleet, and returns to shareholders. Within this framework, Seadrill intends to maintain a net leverage target of less than 1.0x under current market conditions, with a maximum through-cycle net leverage target of less than 2.0x. Seadrill also intends to maintain a strong liquidity position to provide resilience even in a downturn scenario by establishing a target minimum cash-on-hand of $250 million. Further, Seadrill intends to evaluate the potential for accretive additions in core asset categories. </span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">So long as Seadrill is able to meet its net leverage and liquidity targets on a forward-looking basis, as well as comply with its Revolving Credit Facility covenant requirements, Seadrill would seek to provide a return to our shareholders of at least 50% of Free Cash Flow (defined as cash flows from operating activities minus additions to drilling units and equipment) in the form of Share repurchases or dividends. Seadrill will consider additional returns to shareholders from the proceeds of any asset sales in the absence of identified, accretive opportunities. Dividends and Share repurchases will be authorized and determined by the Board of Directors in its sole discretion and depend upon a number of factors, including those described above, its future prospects, market trend evaluation and such other factors as the Board of Directors may deem relevant. Please see Item 1A. "Risk Factors - Financial and Tax Risks - </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">We may be unable to meet our capital allocation framework goal of returning at least 50% of Free Cash Flow to shareholders through dividends and share repurchases, which could decrease expected returns on an investment in our Shares</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">" in Part I of our 2025 10-K.</span></div><div><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">During the second quarter of 2024, the Company's Board of Directors authorized a $500 million Share repurchase program that will run for a period of two years from June 25, 2024, the date of completion for the programs initiated in 2023 ("</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:112%">Current Repurchase Program</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">"). Under the Current Repurchase Program, the Board authorized the Company to purchase up to $300 million of the Company's Shares in 2024. Since the Current Repurchase Program commenced, the Company has repurchased an aggregate of 6,714,252 Shares with a weighted average Share price of $43.52, amounting to $292&#160;million. On September 30, 2024 and December 16, 2024, the Company canceled 4,213,349 and 2,500,903 treasury Shares, respectively, repurchased under this program.</span></div><div><span><br/></span></div><div><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">During the</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:120%"> </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">three</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:700;line-height:120%"> </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">months ended March&#160;31, 2026 and March&#160;31, 2025, the Company did not repurchase Shares.</span></div><div><span><br/></span></div><div><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">As of March&#160;31, 2026, $208&#160;million of the $500 million authorized amount remained available under the Current Repurchase Program.</span></div><div><span><br/></span></div><div><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">While the Current Repurchase Program has a fixed expiration, it may be modified, suspended or discontinued at any time. Shares may be repurchased at any time and from time to time under the program in open market purchases, privately negotiated purchases, block trades, tender offers, accelerated share repurchase transactions or other derivative transactions, through the purchase of call options or the sale of put options, or otherwise, or by any combination of the foregoing. The Company is under no obligation to purchase any Shares in respect of the repurchase program. The manner, timing, pricing and amount of any repurchases may be based upon a number of factors, including market conditions, the Company&#8217;s financial position and capital requirements, financial conditions, competing uses for cash, statutory solvency requirements, the restrictions in the Company&#8217;s debt agreements and other factors.</span></div><div><span><br/></span></div><div><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">The Company may continue Share repurchases pursuant to the Current Repurchase Program at the Board&#8217;s discretion. While we intend to announce the initiation of any Board approved repurchase programs in the future, as well as periodic information required under U.S. securities laws and regulations, we do not intend to announce any sub-authorizations for Share repurchases made pursuant to the Current Repurchase Program or any successor program given that we are no longer required to comply with European regulations requiring onerous disclosure in connection with repurchase programs.</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">2) Liquidity</span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">Our level of liquidity fluctuates depending on a number of factors. These include, among others, our drilling units being on contract, economic utilization achieved, average contract dayrates, timing of accounts receivable collection, capital expenditures for rig upgrades and reactivation projects and timing of payments for operating costs and other obligations. </span></div><div style="margin-bottom:6pt;margin-top:6pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:9pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">As of March&#160;31, 2026, Seadrill had available liquidity of $482&#160;million, which consisted of unrestricted cash of $304&#160;million and available borrowings under our Revolving Credit Facility of $178&#160;million. Our cash on hand, available borrowings under the Revolving Credit Facility, and contract and other revenues are expected to generate sufficient cash flow to fund our anticipated debt service and working capital requirements for the next 12 months.</span></div><div style="height:54pt;position:relative;width:100%"><div style="bottom:0;position:absolute;width:100%"><div
